In order to work efficiently, our phase classification algorithms require a
local
operator whose expectation value (a) has the same sign as that of
O_{L}
; and (b) is above or below a margin (here,
1/2
), in order to determine the required accuracy of the classical shadows.
Recently, criterion (a) was explicitly demonstrated by Tasaki
tasakiPRL2018
;
Tasaki2020
using a local version
O_{\ell}
of the twist, see Eq. (
256
) below. We collect relevant parts of his results to prove both criteria in the theorem below.
Due to the existence of a local operator for classifying the SPT phases, our ML algorithms are guaranteed to predict the SPT phases accurately based on the proof given in Appendix
J
.

Theorem 8
.

Consider the triple
\left\{H(x),\ket{\psi(x)},\Delta(x)\right\}
containing

(2L+2)
-site spin-one chains with periodic boundary conditions

H(x)=\sum_{j=-(L-r)}^{L-r+1}h_{j}(x)+h_{-L}(x)+h_{L+1}(x)

(252)

that admit corresponding unique ground states
\ket{\psi(x)}
and spectral
gaps
\Delta(x)\geq\gamma=\Omega(1)
,
bounded interaction strength
\left\lVert h_{j}(x)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq R=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
and whose terms
h_{j}(x)
are supported on sites
k
such that
|j-k|\leq r=\mathcal{O}(1)
.
Assume that
H(x)
is
O(2)
-symmetric, with the symmetry group
generated by

1.

a collective
z
-axis rotation by any angle, and

2.

an
x
-axis rotation by
\pi
.

There exists a few-body observable
A
, such that for all
x
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{sign}(\bra{\psi(x)}A\ket{\psi(x)})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{sign}\left(\bra{\psi(x)}O_{L}\ket{\psi(x)}\right),\quad\text{as well as}

(253a)

\displaystyle|\bra{\psi(x)}A\ket{\psi(x)}|

\displaystyle\geq 1/2\penalty\ .

(253b)

Proof.

We use spin-one operators
S^{(\alpha)}
with
\alpha\in\{x,y,z\}
that
have eigenvalues
\{0,\pm 1\}
and satisfy angular-momentum commutation
relations
[S^{(x)},S^{(y)}]=\mathrm{i}S^{(z)}
. Eigenstates of
S^{(z)}

are denoted by
|\sigma\rangle
with
\sigma\in\{0,\pm 1\}
. A rotation
around axis
\alpha
is a unitary operator generated by the corresponding

S^{(\alpha)}
. The two symmetry group generators are, for
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
,

U\left(\theta\right)=\bigotimes_{j=-L}^{L+1}e^{-\mathrm{i}\theta S_{j}^{(z)}}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,V=\bigotimes_{j=-L}^{L+1}e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi S_{j}^{(x)}}\,.

(254)

By assumption, both symmetries commute with each Hamiltonian term

h_{j}
; we will explicitly use both to prove the theorem. We will also need superimposed versions
S^{(\pm)}=S^{(x)}\pm\mathrm{i}S^{(y)}
,
which satisfy

e^{\mathrm{i}\phi S^{(z)}}S^{(\pm)}e^{-\mathrm{i}\phi S^{(z)}}=S^{(\pm)}e^{\pm\mathrm{i}\phi}\,.

(255)

The family of unitary twist operators
Affleck1986a
, acting on an interval of
2\ell
spins centered at the origin, is

\displaystyle O_{\ell}=\bigotimes_{k\,,\,\left|k-\tfrac{1}{2}\right|\leq\ell+\tfrac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-\mathrm{i}2\pi\frac{k+\ell}{2\ell+1}S_{k}^{(z)}\right)\,.

(256)

Each site’s rotation is by a multiple of
2\pi/(2\ell+1)
that is
proportional to the site index, forming the namesake twist pattern. The
\ell=L
case reduces to the aforementioned nonlocal twist operator
O_{L}
, while
\ell\ll L
are its local versions.

Suppressing
x
dependence, the key property is that the twisted
ground state
O_{\ell}|\psi\rangle
has energy close to that of the ground state.
In particular, there exists
C_{0},C_{1}>0
, such that for all
\ell\geq C_{0}
, Lemma
12
below yields

\langle\psi|O_{\ell}HO_{\ell}^{\dagger}|\psi\rangle-\langle\psi|H|\psi\rangle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\ell}\,.

(257)

The ground state is unique by our assumption of a gap, so the twisted ground state must then become proportional to the ground state as
\ell\to\infty
. In other
words, the magnitude of their overlap must be close to one as long as
\ell\geq C_{0}
,

\left|\langle\psi|O_{\ell}|\psi\rangle\right|^{2}\geq 1-\frac{C_{1}}{\Delta\ell}\,;

(258)

see Lemma
13
below.
The phase of this overlap is either
0
or
\pi
because the
\pi
-rotation

V
leaves the ground state invariant:

\langle\psi|O_{\ell}|\psi\rangle=\langle\psi|V^{\dagger}O_{\ell}V|\psi\rangle=\langle\psi|O_{\ell}^{\dagger}|\psi\rangle=\overline{\langle\psi|O_{\ell}|\psi\rangle}\in\mathbb{R}\,.

(259)

Hence, the few-body Hermitian observable

A=(O_{\ell}+O^{\dagger}_{\ell})/2
with
\ell=\max(4\gamma/(3C_{1}),C_{0})
satisfies

|\langle\psi|A|\psi\rangle|=|\langle\psi|O_{\ell}|\psi\rangle|\geq\sqrt{1-\frac{C_{1}}{\Delta\ell}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\penalty\ ,

(260)

proving Eq. (
253b
). Note that the required value of
\ell
depends on the gap, and thus also on
x
.

To prove Eq. (
253a
), we need to show that the sign of the twist’s expectation value remains the same for any
\ell\geq\max(4\gamma/(3C_{1}),C_{0})
. To do this, first notice that, when
\ell
is relaxed to be a nonnegative real, the twist (
256
) is
continuous
in
\ell
. (This can be verified, e.g., by studying the twist’s eigenvalues.)
Continuity implies that the expectation value cannot change sign; otherwise, it would have to cross zero, thus violating Eq. (
260
). Therefore, the sign remains the same, confirming Eq. (
253a
). Similarly, by continuity in
\ell
and
x
, the expectation value maintains its sign within each phase.
∎

The above argument is contingent on two auxiliary statements, which we now prove.

Lemma 12

(Vanishing energy difference
tasakiPRL2018
; Eq. (
257
))
.

For constants
C_{0},C_{1}
, as long as
\ell\geq C_{0}
, we have

\langle\psi|O_{\ell}HO_{\ell}^{\dagger}|\psi\rangle-\langle\psi|H|\psi\rangle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\ell}.

(261)

Proof.

Using the variational principle (which says that the difference in energy between any state and the ground state is nonnegative), plugging in
O_{\ell}
and
H
, applying

\langle\psi|O|\psi\rangle\leq\left\|O\right\|_{\infty}
,
and distributing the norm over the sum yields

\displaystyle\langle\psi|O_{\ell}HO_{\ell}^{\dagger}|\psi\rangle-\langle\psi|H|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\langle\psi|\left(O_{\ell}HO_{\ell}^{\dagger}+O_{\ell}^{\dagger}HO_{\ell}-2H\right)|\psi\rangle

(262a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=-\left(\ell+r\right)}^{\ell+r+1}\langle\psi|\left(O_{\ell}h_{j}O_{\ell}^{\dagger}+O_{\ell}^{\dagger}h_{j}O_{\ell}-2h_{j}\right)|\psi\rangle

(262b)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=-\left(\ell+r\right)}^{\ell+r+1}\left\|O_{\ell}h_{j}O_{\ell}^{\dagger}+O_{\ell}^{\dagger}h_{j}O_{\ell}-2h_{j}\right\|_{\infty}

(262c)

Next, we use the finite support and rotational
invariance of
h_{j}
from Eq. (
254
) to rotate the
twist
O_{\ell}
,

\displaystyle O_{\ell}h_{j}O_{\ell}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=O_{\ell}U\left(\theta_{j}\right)h_{j}U^{\dagger}\left(\theta_{j}\right)O_{\ell}^{\dagger}

(263a)

\displaystyle=\left(\bigotimes_{\left|k-j\right|\leq r}e^{-\mathrm{i}\left(\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}\left[k+\ell\right]+\theta_{j}\right)S_{k}^{(z)}}\right)h_{j}\left(\bigotimes_{\left|k-j\right|\leq r}e^{\mathrm{i}\left(\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}\left[k+\ell\right]+\theta_{j}\right)S_{k}^{(z)}}\right)\,,

(263b)

where we pick
\theta_{j}=-\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}\left(j+\ell\right)

for each
j
. That way, the twist does not affect site
j
, with

\displaystyle O_{\ell}h_{j}O_{\ell}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}M_{j}}h_{j}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}M_{j}}\,,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\text{and}\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,M_{j}=\sum_{|k-j|\leq r}\left(j-k\right)S_{k}^{(z)}\,.

(264)

We now expand
h_{j}
as a polynomial in
\{S_{k}^{(z)},S_{k}^{(\pm)}\}
. This can be done because products of powers of these operators form a matrix basis for any operator on the chain. For a single site, the set
\{S^{(z)}S^{(\pm)},(S^{(+)})^{2}\}
, along with their complex conjugates and some powers of
S^{(z)}
, form the basis of nine matrix units for all
3\times 3
operators on the site. Tensor products of these operators therefore form a matrix-unit basis for all sites. The conjugation property (
255
) and Eq. (
264
) imply that each term in the expansion of
h_{j}
, upon conjugation by
O_{\ell}
, will be imparted with a phase that is some multiple
\mu
of
2\pi/(2\ell+1)
. Combining all terms with the same phase into
h_{j,\mu}
, we have

e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}M_{j}}h_{j,\mu}e^{-\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}M_{j}}=h_{j,\mu}e^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}\mu}\,.

(265)

Moreover,
|\mu|\leq 2\mu_{\text{max}}
, where
\mu_{\text{max}}=\sum_{\left|k-j\right|\leq r}\left|j-k\right|=r\left(r+1\right)

is the largest eigenvalue of
M_{j}
. Plugging this in and expanding
the resulting cosine yields

\displaystyle\left\|O_{\ell}h_{j}O_{\ell}^{\dagger}+O_{\ell}^{\dagger}h_{j}O_{\ell}-2h_{j}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=2\left\|\sum_{\left|\mu\right|\leq 2r\left(r+1\right)}\left[\cos\left(\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}\mu\right)-1\right]h_{j,\mu}\right\|_{\infty}

(266a)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2\pi}{2\ell+1}\right)^{2}\sum_{\left|\mu\right|\leq 2r\left(r+1\right)}\mu^{2}\left\|h_{j,\mu}\right\|_{\infty}\,.

(266b)

Since the spin operators form a matrix-unit basis, each
h_{j,\mu}
is simply
h_{j}
with some entries removed. Therefore, the norm of
h_{j,\mu}
is bounded by
R
. Applying that and performing the remaining sum (
262c
) over

j
yields

\displaystyle\langle\psi|O_{\ell}HO_{\ell}^{\dagger}|\psi\rangle-\langle\psi|H|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\ell+r+1}{\left(2\ell+1\right)^{2}}4\pi^{2}R\left(\sum_{\left|\mu\right|\leq 2r\left(r+1\right)}\mu^{2}\right)\,.

(267)

Thus, for
\ell\geq C_{0}
, the difference in energies between
the ground state and twisted ground state will be bounded by
C_{1}/\ell
,
where
C_{0},C_{1}
are two constants depending on the interaction range
r
and norm bound
R
of the Hamiltonian terms.
∎

Lemma 13

(High overlap
Tasaki2020
; Eq. (
258
))
.

For constants
C_{0},C_{1}
, as long as
\ell\geq C_{0}
, we have

\left|\langle\psi|O_{\ell}|\psi\rangle\right|^{2}\geq 1-\frac{C_{1}}{\Delta\ell}\,.

(268)

Proof.

All eigenvalues
of
H
are bounded below by the sum of the ground state energy
E_{\text{gnd}}=\langle\psi|H|\psi\rangle

and spectral gap
\Delta
,

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle\geq E_{\text{gnd}}|\psi\rangle\!\langle\psi|+\left(E_{\text{gnd}}+\Delta\right)\left(\mathbb{I}-|\psi\rangle\!\langle\psi|\right)=E_{\text{gnd}}\mathbb{I}+\Delta\left(\mathbb{I}-|\psi\rangle\!\langle\psi|\right)\,.

(269)

Conjugating by
O_{\ell}
and evaluating the result in the ground
state yields

\displaystyle\langle\psi|O_{\ell}HO_{\ell}^{\dagger}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\geq E_{\text{gnd}}+\Delta\left(1-\left|\langle\psi|O_{\ell}|\psi\rangle\right|^{2}\right)\,.

(270)

Rearranging this and plugging in Lemma
12
yields
the desired result.
∎
